Handling Edge cases ^NqXdToe4

1. In this case array were of equal size sometimes the division would not be of same size 
2. In that case one array would remain with elements which are not processed.
3. We will add those elements directly at end of array. ^2K276nHg

Why Aren't we sorting them ?  ^X7YHK8uZ

1. If we look closely this is recursive approach so if we reach a point like [3,4] and [1,5,6] 
we can see that both are sorted in their own way.
2. That is why 6 from array B would be just appended into Arr Final without sorting. ^h1cGkOwG

Finally we return the arrFinal array  ^6ztSKUYS

def mergeSort(nums: Array[int], start, end) returns sorted Array[int] {
    if nums.length == 1 {
        return nums
    }
    midOfArray = nums.length / 2
    leftArray = mergeSort(nums, 0, midofArray)
    rightArray = mergeSort(nums, mid, nums.length)
    return merge(leftArray, rightArray)
}

def merge(first: Array[int], second: Array[int]) returns new Array[int] {
    finalArray = [] * (first.length + second.length)
    i = 0
    j = 0
    k = 0
    while i < first.length && j < second.length {
        if first[i] < second[j] {
            finalArray[k] = first[i]
            i = i + 1
        }else{
            finalArray[k] = second[j]
            j = j + 1
        }
        k = k + 1
    }

    while i < first.length {
        finalArray[k] = first[i]
        i = i + 1
        k = k + 1
    }

    while j < second.length {
        finalArray[k] = second[j]
        j = j + 1
        k = k + 1
    }

    return finalArray
}
 ^feq35hzr
